---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# I03 Historical-opponent ecology, first rung: assay sensitivity. Slice plan (v1)

Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-01. Branch `claude/i03-historical-opponents` from `origin/main` 67fdd025. Source: "I03" and "Agent execution contract" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; coordinator decisions D1, D7, D10, D12 (the log in this checkout ends at D10, so D12 text is unverified here; D10 says I03 follows I02 fixtures, and this slice uses only committed founders and the generalist, so it has no I02 dependency; D7 requires rebase and focused revalidation before publication, see section 6). Follows the I14 slice layout (`docs/predictability-atlas-2026-11-01/`, merged as D9). Observer and tooling only. No physics change, no model calls, no GPU, no deploy. CPU only (sim-ref), and every artifact says so.

## 1. Bounded question

Can a genome-only, standard-inoculum, mutation-off pairwise contest on small `sim-ref` arenas tell apart a few known-distinct strategies, and does it correctly stay quiet where it should? Concretely: (a) a deliberately weakened variant must be detected as losing to its parent (positive control); (b) the same genome under two lineage labels must show exact label invariance and no significant position bias (null control); (c) runs where both sides sit at the extinction floor must be reported uninformative, not as ties; (d) solo growth at the same positions is reported beside each payoff, as a descriptive monoculture residual (not a causal separation of vigour from matchup effects); (e) an intransitive cycle must be detectable when one is constructed, and reported as "none detected" otherwise.

This is the sensitivity check the report requires before any tournament. It answers nothing about evolution over time, open-endedness, predation or organism reproduction, and it is not the M3 environment (small `sim-ref` worlds, one fixed chemistry). If (a) or (b) fails or (c) dominates, the honest result is "assay uninformative or biased"; that is a valid, shippable outcome and the PR says so.

## 2. What is built (one PR)

1. **Evidence fixture with negative cases that fool the naive metric.** The naive metric is "who has more biomass at the end of one run" (win rate over single runs, 0/0 read as a tie). Negative cases, each built so the naive reading is wrong and the sealed rule is right:
   - `twin-labels`: genome G under labels G and G'. Naive: every single run has a "winner" by share, so a naive win-rate view of individual runs looks decisive; the sealed rule (exact label invariance, then signed position effect across seed blocks with a sign-flip test) must show no significant bias. The evidence also reports the naive run-level "decisive winner" rate (share beyond 0.5 +- 0.10) for the twins so the gap is visible.
   - `depletion-arena`: an in-world configuration chosen in calibration (plain nutrient 0 is not assumed to deplete; see 3.5 G4) in which both sides reach the extinction floor. Naive: share 0/0 -> 0.5 -> "balanced, assay clean". Sealed rule: class `both-floor`, pair status `uninformative`, excluded from payoffs, counted; if calibration finds no such arena, a pure classifier fixture stands in and the evidence says so.
   - `vigour-only` (pure-function fixture, arithmetic only): solo growth 3:1, payoff exactly the vigour share. Naive: "A dominates B". Sealed rule: residual 0. It does not validate the in-world residual.
   - `rps-synthetic` (pure-function fixture): a constructed 3x3 payoff matrix with a known A>B>C>A cycle, plus a transitive matrix. Detector must find exactly the one cycle and none in the transitive one. Labelled synthetic in evidence and on the page; the real panel reports whatever it finds (expected: none, stated as "none detected at this resolution", never "none exists").
2. **Small CPU path.** Pure TypeScript, integer-only for anything touching sim state; floats only in the final summary statistics (payoff means, intervals). Library `packages/metrics/src/opponents/`, CLI `tools/opponents/assay.ts` (Node 24, relative imports, like `tools/predictability/atlas.ts`) running contests across `worker_threads`. Reuses `buildWorld`/`Founder` (schema), `RefSim` (sim-ref), `generalistGenome`, `M3_FOUNDERS`/`founderGenome`, `genomeDistance` (schema), `mean`/`sd` (metrics stats). No new package, so no alias-map edits.
3. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence** `evidence.json` (sealed, canonical JSON, no timestamps or platform strings) and `manifest.json` (source hashes, config, seeds, budget), with `--write`, `--check` (no simulation) and `--replay-check` (regenerate, require byte equality), same contract as the predictability CLI.
4. **Local prototype page** `apps/lab/opponents/index.html` plus `apps/lab/src/opponents/{page.ts,render.ts,style.css}`: payoff matrix with interval text, a "contest time" selector (matrix at steps 100/200/300/400, so you can see a matchup resolve or not; the history axis of the report's time-versus-opponent view needs an archive and is deferred), ratio and position toggles, a vigour-vs-matchup toggle, extinction counts, the controls. Kept off the deployed site exactly like `/predictability/`.

## 3. Design

### 3.0 Reuse decision (review finding 1)

`packages/runner/src/discovery.ts` already freezes genome-only founder fixtures (`FixtureSpec`, `buildInitialState` over `buildWorld`), runs CPU cases with a deadline and replay validation, and the report points at it. Decision: **adopt its fixture vocabulary, not its campaign runner.** Contest worlds are described as founder specs with `genomeHex` (same JSON shape as `FixtureSpec` `initial.kind: "founders"`) so a later rung can port them into a discovery campaign unchanged. The runner itself is not reused because (i) its observer ledger is capability/census oriented and has no per-lineage payoff observer, and adding one is a shared `packages/runner` and schema change with its own versioning, which this observer-only slice must avoid; (ii) it writes a checkpoint artifact per case, which is heavy for ~800 short throwaway contests. Work order step 0 is a one-hour spike that reads `discovery.ts` and records in `EVIDENCE.md` the concrete incompatibilities found (or, if the spike shows a cheap observer hook, switches to it and the plan is amended). `tools/assay.ts` / `tools/foundations.ts` time-shift machinery and `tools/lib/pond-assay.ts` (64x64 ponds hardcoded; budget-accounting precedent only) are read for design and not reused; `packages/lineage` mutation replay is deferred (mutation off, fixed founders).

### 3.1 Arena and standard inoculum (frozen before any evidence run)

- Config: `defaultConfig({seed, tileW, tileH, mutRate: 0})`, single tile (size from calibration grid), everything else default. No new `WorldConfig` keys; `defaultConfig()` untouched.
- Slots: four slots at fixed lattice centres (a quarter and three quarters of the tile in each axis), jittered per axis by up to +-2 cells. Jitter value = `lowbias32((seed ^ imul(slot + 1, 0x9E3779B1) ^ imul(axis + 1, 0x85EBCA6B) ^ SALT_JITTER) >>> 0)` mod 5 minus 2 (`lowbias32` takes ONE argument; a two-argument call is a TS error and JS ignores the second). A test pins distinct values per slot and axis and reproducibility. Calibration asserts toroidal non-overlap of discs including worst-case jitter (centre separation minus 4 > 2R).
- Founder array is always the full four slots in slot order, so `buildWorld`'s per-founder and per-cell noise is identical across all arenas that share a seed. A slot a given arena does not use is emptied after `buildWorld` by zeroing that slot's disc cells (B, E, genome channels; nutrient left as built). `biomass 256 / energy 512 / nutrient 256` are planting parameters: `buildWorld` draws per-cell biomass in `0..2*biomass` and derives energy from the draw, so actual step-zero inventories are measured, recorded per label in the evidence, and used as the initial share. Test: step-zero inventory of a slot is equal across every contest and solo arena at a seed.
- Arenas, with slot sets fixed (A on set X, B on the rest):
  - **balanced**: A on {0,1}, B on {2,3} (position P1) and A on {2,3}, B on {0,1} (P2).
  - **rare-A**: A on {0} vs B on {1,2,3} (position R1) and A on {2} vs B on {0,1,3} (R2).
  - **rare-B**: the same with roles exchanged. Rare-A and rare-B are separate arms; they are never averaged into a payoff, and neutral expectation is the measured initial share, not 0.5.
  - **solo**: each mask used above, occupied by a single genotype and the other slots emptied (masks {0,1}, {2,3}, {0}, {2}, {1,2,3}, {0,1,3}), so vigour is measured at the actual disc count and positions.
- Lineage labels: `buildWorld` gives slot `i` lineage raw id `i+1`; a label map takes lineage id -> genotype label (twins G and G' share a genome and differ in label only). With `mutRate 0` no other lineage ids may appear; asserted at every checkpoint (every living cell's lineage is a founder id; labelled biomass sums to total biomass).
- Observation is read-only on `RefSim.state` at the checkpoints; a test steps the same world with and without readouts and requires equal `stateHash`.
- The seed block (`cfg.seed` plus its jitter) is the independent unit. All matchups reuse the same seed blocks (paired design); that is legitimate pairing and is never counted as extra replication.

### 3.2 Readouts per run (integer)

`bA`, `bB` = sum of CH.B per label; `cellsA`, `cellsB`; all stored as integers. Shares are computed from the integer counts in floating point at aggregation time; `shareA_ppm = floor(1e6*bA/(bA+bB))` is a display value only and never feeds a gate (review finding 2: flooring turned an exact 0.5 reciprocal mean into 0.4999995). Floor: a label is at the floor when its biomass is below `FLOOR_FRAC = 1/16` of its own measured step-zero biomass; `FLOOR_FRAC` is frozen now and not a calibration parameter. Run class: `contested` (both above floor), `decisive` (exactly one at floor; informative, a win), `both-floor` (uninformative; share is undefined, never 0.5).

### 3.3 Panel (genetically separated founders)

Five genotypes, sealed by hex in the manifest:
- `G` hand-built generalist (`generalistGenome(defaultMu, defaultSigma)`).
- `F1..F3` three M3 founders chosen by a deterministic farthest-point rule on `genomeDistance` starting from G (`M3_FOUNDER_SET` id recorded). `genomeDistance` counts differing slots; it shows the genotypes are separated, not that they follow different competitive strategies in these arenas (M3 qualification concerns survival, repair and light dependence in another assay). The plan therefore says "genetically separated founders" everywhere; functional spread is reported descriptively from solo vigour and the payoff matrix, and no verdict claims strategy discrimination beyond the controls actually validated.
- `W` weakened G: photosynthesis output weight (`w2(1, OUT.PHOTO)`) reduced by an amount from the calibration grid. Reducing it also lowers catalytic work cost, so a loss is not guaranteed by the edit; calibration must show W loses with W's solo vigour above the floor (so a loss is not just death).
- `G'` same genome as G under a second label: the genotype-neutral control, not a panel member.
Contests: 10 panel pairs; controls `G` vs `G'` (same arms) and the depletion control (3.5, G4). `F1` vs `F1'` is optional (first cut).

### 3.4 Aggregation and inference (post-processing, floats allowed)

- Eligibility: a pair's seed block is **complete** only if both reciprocal positions are non-`both-floor` at the primary checkpoint. Incomplete blocks are dropped whole (never keep a surviving single position), counted and shown. Pair status `uninformative` if complete blocks < 80% of seeds (8 of 10; 5 of 6 for rare arms). Report a naive payoff (both-floor counted 0.5, incomplete blocks kept) beside it so the effect of exclusion is visible. Fixtures cover asymmetric exclusion.
- Seeds: S = 10 evidence seeds (101..110) for the balanced arm; rare arms use the first 6 (101..106) and are **exploratory**. Calibration seeds are 1..3 only and never reused.
- **Primary analysis (confirmatory, the only inferential claims):** the final checkpoint T, balanced arm, payoff per complete block = mean over P1 and P2 of A's share computed from integer counts. Gates G1/G2 and cycle edges use these. Inference is an exact sign-flip test on block payoff minus 0.5 (min two-sided p = 2/2^n, so 0.002 at n = 10; with n = 6 it would be 0.031 and could never survive a family of 10 pairs, which is why S = 10). A directed edge A->B requires: all complete blocks on the same side of 0.5 (p <= 2/2^n, which must be <= 0.05/10 for the 10-pair family, so n >= 9 or the edge is not allowed) AND mean offset >= 0.05. The t interval (df = retained blocks - 1, frozen t table) is shown as a descriptive uncertainty band only, with the caveat that payoffs are bounded and may be bimodal; it never decides anything.
- **Secondary/exploratory (labelled as such in evidence and page):** other checkpoints (100/200/300 for the contest-time view), rare arms, invasion, vigour residual, breadth. No multiplicity correction is claimed for them and none of them feeds the verdict.
- **Position effect** is gated separately from reciprocal averaging (review finding 2): per block, the signed slot-set advantage (share of the label on slot set {0,1} in P1 minus its initial share) and its absolute size are reported before averaging; for the twin control this is the whole bias measurement.
- **Invasion from rare:** from rare-A and rare-B arms separately, rare label's final share minus its measured initial share per block (paired), with the sign-flip test over the 6 blocks; classes `invades`, `fails`, `unclear` (6 blocks cannot reach a family-wise bar, so `invades`/`fails` need all 6 blocks same sign and are labelled exploratory). Mutual invasibility is "both invade", no mechanism claimed.
- **Vigour (descriptive monoculture residual, not a causal separation):** solo biomass `V_x(mask)` at the identical mask and step; vigour-expected share `V_A/(V_A+V_B)`; residual = observed share minus expected share. The residual can still contain density response and spatial self-interaction (affinity depends on local B+P; metabolism on local resources), so it is described as "share not predicted by solo growth at the same positions", with that limitation stated, and no gate depends on it. The hand-built `vigour-only` fixture tests arithmetic only.
- **Cycles:** over the confirmatory edges only; all directed 3-cycles and a transitivity check. Mean payoff and breadth (opponents beaten) are reported separately, never combined.
- **Extinction counts:** per run class, per pair, per ratio, per checkpoint.

### 3.5 Controls and pre-stated verdict rules (written before any evidence run)

- `G1a label invariance (exact)`: swapping the label map on a twin world changes nothing physical; checked as an exact identity of integer counts (`bA` under map1 equals `bB` under the swapped map).
- `G1b twin position bias`: for `G` vs `G'` balanced, the signed slot-set advantage across blocks has an exact sign-flip p > 0.05 AND the mean absolute position effect is below the effect target stated in `calibration.json` (set from the calibration seeds, committed before evidence, and at most half the W-versus-G margin). Same for rare arms on excess share (exploratory, reported, not gating).
- `G2 positive control`: `W` vs `G` complete blocks all below 0.5 (sign-flip p = 0.002 at n = 10) and mean payoff <= 0.40 at the final checkpoint.
- `G3 floor`: at the primary checkpoint, the `both-floor` share of contests is <= 20% in EACH panel pair and each arm (not pooled), no panel pair `uninformative`.
- `G4 depletion`: an in-world depletion arena (configuration chosen in calibration from a bounded list; plain `nutrient 0` is not assumed to deplete because decomposition and abiotic recycling return C to A and biomass/energy persist; a diagnostic with `nutrient 0` retained most inoculum biomass at step 400) must show raw inventories of BOTH labels below the floor at T in every evidence seed, and its pair status must be `uninformative`. If calibration finds no cheap configuration that does so, G4 falls back to a pure classifier fixture on hand-built states and the evidence states "both-floor path not exercised in-world".
- `G5 pure-function fixtures`: `vigour-only` arithmetic, `rps-synthetic` finds exactly one cycle, transitive matrix finds none, straddling-0.5 matrix finds no edge, asymmetric-exclusion fixtures behave as 3.4.
- Verdict decision table (precedence in this order): any of G4, G5 failed or any run invalid/incomplete -> `control-invalid`; G1a or G1b failed -> `label-or-position-biased`; G2 failed -> `insensitive`; G3 failed -> `extinction-limited` (no tournament); otherwise `assay-sensitive`, defined as exactly: "detects the constructed weakening at this effect size and stays quiet for identical genomes at this resolution, on genetically separated founders in small CPU arenas". It does not assert discrimination between real strategies. A non-sensitive verdict is shipped as is. `--check` recomputes every summary and gate from the raw per-block records and compares with the stored values and verdict (not just recomputing the verdict from stored gate booleans). No score selects anything or tunes the assay after evidence seeds are run.

### 3.6 Time conventions

"Time" here is contest time (checkpoints inside one run). No archive and no historical axis exists yet. Archived-generation opponents, withheld-opponent generality and evolving-against-archive comparisons are later rungs.

### 3.7 Calibration stage (before evidence; committed as `calibration.json`)

Bounded, predeclared grid; all attempts are logged and committed, not just the winner: disc radius R in {2,3,4}; tile {32, 40}; T in {300, 400, 600} (checkpoints at T/4, T/2, 3T/4, T); W weakening in {-25%, -50%, -75%} of the photosynthesis output weight; depletion configurations from a short listed set. Enumerate settings in cost order (smallest tile, smallest R, shortest T first) and pick the first that meets the attainability criteria on seeds 1..3: W loses to G with mean payoff <= 0.40 and W's solo vigour above the floor; twin mean absolute position effect below half the W margin; both-floor share on panel pairs <= 20%; depletion arena reaches the floor if achievable. At most 36 attempted settings. Freeze choices in `arena.ts` constants and `manifest.json` in one commit before the first evidence run. `FLOOR_FRAC` and the 0.40 / 20% / 0.05 thresholds are not tuned. If no setting attains the criteria, stop and report "assay cannot be made informative on these arenas".

Job schedule (frozen in the manifest, counted in the evidence): balanced pairs 10 x 10 seeds x 2 positions = 200; rare arms 10 pairs x 6 seeds x 2 arms x 2 positions = 240; twin G/G' balanced and rare = 10x2 + 6x4 = 44; solo 5 genotypes x (2 balanced masks x 10 seeds + 4 rare masks x 6 seeds) = 220, plus W solo; depletion arena 10 seeds x 2; roughly 750-800 runs. Each run reads all checkpoints from one pass. A prototype measured ~30 ms/step at 64x64 serial, so a 32x32 arena is ~7 ms/step and a 400-step run ~3 s; ~800 runs is ~40 min serial and about 10 min on 4 workers, which exceeds the budget, so the budget is *not* assumed met. A separate four-worker 400-step micro-benchmark on this machine finished in 2.78 s, which is not a qualification of the frozen schedule. Calibration benchmarks the exact frozen schedule; if `--replay-check` projects over about 5 minutes, cut in this order (each cut removes jobs, unlike UI cuts): drop the optional F1 twin; reduce rare arms to one position (R1) and 4 seeds; shorten T; reduce balanced S only if edges stay allowed (n >= 9), otherwise drop edge/cycle claims on the real panel and keep the fixture. Arena size is last.

## 5. Tests (vitest, named cases)

`packages/metrics/test/opponents.test.ts`
- `inoculum is identical across contests`: every contest and solo run at fixed seed has the same disc count, biomass and energy totals; ratio 1:3 swaps conserve totals.
- `reciprocal positions swap slot sets`: swapping labels A/B reproduces the same world with labels exchanged (state hash equal after relabelling).
- `mutation off: no new lineage ids`: 400 steps, every living cell's lineage in the founder set at each checkpoint.
- `labels sum to total biomass`: labelled biomass equals total CH.B.
- `readouts do not change physics`: instrumented vs plain twin `stateHash` equal.
- `twin genomes under two labels are distinguishable by lineage only`: identical genome words, different lineage ids, map resolves.
- `share arithmetic is integer and exact`: ppm values for hand cases including 0/0 -> class `both-floor` (never 0.5).
- `run classes at the floor`: contested / decisive / both-floor from hand-built biomass triples at the threshold edges.
- `depletion arena is both-floor and the naive reading says tie`: the calibrated depletion arena (or, on fallback, a hand-built both-floor state); sealed class `both-floor`; naive share 0.5 asserted in the same test; raw inventories of both labels asserted below the floor.
- `payoff interval from known blocks`: hand-computed mean and t interval for fixed per-block values.
- `vigour-only fixture has zero residual`: 3:1 vigour, payoff = 0.75, residual 0.
- `cycle detector finds the constructed cycle only`: `rps-synthetic` yields exactly one cycle (as a set of three), the transitive matrix yields none, a matrix with an interval straddling 0.5 yields no edge.
- `invasion from rare labels`: hand blocks give `invades`, `fails`, `unclear`.
- `breadth is separate from mean payoff`: two genotypes with equal mean payoff and different breadth.
- `jitter is reproducible and differs per slot and axis` (one-argument `lowbias32`).
- `step-zero inventory of a slot is equal across contests and solo arenas`; `reciprocal arenas have equal resource fields`.
- `twin: label swap gives exact count identity; flooring never creates 0.4999995` (ppm display only).
- `complete-block eligibility drops the whole block when one position is both-floor`.
- `rare arms: neutral expectation is the measured initial share`.
- `sign-flip p values: n=6 min 0.031, n=10 min 0.002; edges refused when n < 9`.
- `verdict decision table is exhaustive` (every gate combination incl. G4/G5 failed -> control-invalid).
- `farthest-point pick is deterministic and distinct`: picks stable, pairwise `genomeDistance` above the clustering distance.
- `weakened variant differs from parent only in the stated weight`.
- `verdict follows gates`: synthetic gate sets give each verdict (`assay-sensitive`, `label-or-position-biased`, `insensitive`, `extinction-limited`) and the precedence order is fixed.

`tools/test/opponents-evidence.test.ts`
- `evidence seal and manifest verify` (`checkSaved()` returns no problems; no `generatedAt`/platform strings; statements include "CPU only", "no GPU evidence", "not the M3 environment").
- `a tampered payload, manifest or source is rejected` (edit a payoff, a manifest hash, one source file, an upstream input such as `generalistGenome` source).
- `workers resolve @bl/* without vitest aliases` (Node 24 subprocess, VITEST unset).
- `a correctly resealed but internally inconsistent payload is rejected` (summary or gate edited, seal and manifest recomputed).
- `worker count does not change output`: a mini config (2 pairs, 2 seeds, T=60) at `--jobs 1` and `--jobs 3` is byte equal.
- `mini regeneration equals itself` (determinism).
- `evidence records seeds, panel hex, distances, calibration constants, excluded counts, and the naive comparison`.
- `verdict in evidence equals the verdict recomputed from the stored gates`.

`apps/lab/test/opponents-packaging.test.ts` (same shape as the predictability one): Vite input present, Dockerfile removal list and Caddy private route (`/opponents`, `/OPPONENTS/index.html` match; `/opponents-other` does not), page text carries "CPU only", "no GPU evidence", "noindex", a captioned `<table>`, and the site build keeps a self-contained asset graph after the Dockerfile deletions.

`apps/lab/test/opponents-render.test.ts`: matrix renders the same numbers as the table; `uninformative` cells are labelled, not coloured as ties; reduced-motion; checkpoint/ratio/position toggles change the displayed numbers; text says synthetic for the cycle fixture.

## 6. Acceptance gates (runnable)

```bash
export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
pnpm install && pnpm typecheck
pnpm vitest run packages/metrics/test/opponents.test.ts tools/test/opponents-evidence.test.ts apps/lab/test/opponents-packaging.test.ts apps/lab/test/opponents-render.test.ts
node tools/opponents/assay.ts --check                 # integrity, no simulation
time node tools/opponents/assay.ts --replay-check     # byte-equal regeneration, under ~5 min
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts   # untouched pins
pnpm test                                             # full suite; report load-sensitive flakes separately
git diff --stat origin/main -- packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu packages/schema experiments   # must be empty
```
Before publication (D7): rebase on current origin/main, rerun the focused tests, `--check` and (if any hashed source changed) `--write` again, re-review the delta.
Plus: the calibration commit precedes the first `--write`; `evidence.json` `verdict` is recorded whatever it is; Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh read-only review of this plan (REVIEW.md, before code) and of the source before commit.

## 7. Page content

Header "Local experiment / historical opponents, first rung". Plain-language option text for ratio ("1:1 even start; 1:3 one side starts rare, which tests invasion"), position ("both positions averaged; the difference is shown as position effect") and contest time. Matrix cells show payoff, interval and excluded count; `uninformative` is its own state. Caption "What this does not show": a sensitivity check on five genetically separated founders in small CPU arenas, not the M3 environment; a payoff is share of biomass at a step, not reproduction or fitness; wins can be vigour, see the vigour toggle; the cycle fixture is synthetic; "none detected" is not "none exist"; no archive tournament has been run; CPU only, no GPU evidence, not on the public site. A real `<table>` carries every number the canvas shows.

## 8. Non-goals and limits

- No historical-archive tournament, no evolving against opponents, no generality-on-withheld-opponents (needs training history), no synthesis-versus-lottery attribution (reported as unresolved, no predation claim), no whole-patch transfer (that moves stored resources and developmental state; different experiment).
- No physics, schema, preset or `RULE_VERSION` change; no GPU or backend qualification; hosted/physical-GPU evidence: none.
- Small `sim-ref` worlds, five genotypes, S=10 seed blocks (6 for rare arms): wide intervals are expected and stated; an interval containing 0.5 is not evidence of equality.
- Weakened variant is a single hand-built manipulation, a sensitivity floor, not a spectrum of effect sizes.
- Fitness here is relative biomass share at a fixed step; it does not establish organism reproduction, open-endedness or ecological context effects.
- No paid calls, AWS, deploy, push or PR at this stage; the plan stage commits nothing.

## 9. Size and order of work

About 600 lines of library, 250 of CLI and worker, 400 of tests, one HTML/CSS/TS page. Order: step 0 discovery.ts spike (3.0); arena + labelling + invariants tests; payoff/cycle/vigour pure functions with fixtures; panel pick; calibration (scratch, freeze constants); CLI + worker + evidence/manifest; page; packaging edits and tests; Codex source review. If scope must shrink, cut in this order: `F1` twin control, see the job-schedule cuts in 3.7 (UI cuts do not save simulation time). Optional arms to cut first: F1 twin, rare-arm positions and seeds, contest-time selector.

# I03 first rung: implementation evidence (2026-11-01)

Branch `claude/i03-historical-opponents` from `origin/main` 67fdd025. Uncommitted. CPU only (sim-ref), no GPU evidence, not the M3 environment.

## Result

`evidence.json` verdict: **assay-sensitive**, defined as: detects the constructed weakening at this effect size and stays quiet for identical genomes at this resolution, on genetically separated founders in small CPU arenas. It does not assert discrimination between real strategies. Seal `9f43b866...7aef` (identical across two independent `--write` runs and `--replay-check`).

Gates (all pass): G1a exact label invariance; G1b twin position effect (10 seeds, mean size 0.066 < target 0.075, sign-flip p = 0.68; per-seed effects range -0.11..+0.20, so single runs are noisy and the pass is near its target); G2 W loses to G in all 10 blocks, W mean share 0.347, W alive alone; G3 no both-floor runs in any pair or arm; G4 in-world depletion arena (dark, kBDecay 600, kELeak 1000) both sides at floor in all 20 runs, status uninformative, naive reading is a 0.50 tie; G5 fixtures.

Real panel (G, three farthest-point M3 founders F1..F3, W): confirmed edges G>F3, G>W (G vs F1 is even, 0.477), F1>F2, F1>F3, F1>W. Cycles: none detected at this resolution (the detector finds the constructed A>B>C>A fixture only). The panel is ordered F1 ~ G > F2 > W ~ F3 by mean payoff; edges are strict (all 10 blocks one side, p <= 0.005, gap >= 0.05).

## Findings worth stating

- Exploratory rare-start (1 disc vs 3): the rare label loses share in nearly every pair, including the identical-genome control (-0.16 mean, all 6 seeds). A lone disc is disadvantaged by arrangement alone, so "fails" classes in the rare arm are not interpretable as failure to invade; read against the twin rare row. Not gated.
- Vigour residual (descriptive): residuals are within about +/-0.09 of solo-growth predictions; the largest is F1 vs F3 (+0.089). No causal separation claimed.
- Calibration: first attainable setting in cost order was attempt 11 (tile 32, R 3, T 300, weaken 50%); the twin target was met on 3 calibration seeds by a thin margin (0.0752 vs 0.0755). All 11 attempts and 3 depletion candidates are in `calibration.json`.

## Step 0 spike (plan 3.0): discovery.ts

`packages/runner/src/discovery.ts` was read for fixture vocabulary only. Its runner is not reused: it has no per-lineage payoff observer (adding one is a shared runner/schema change) and writes a checkpoint artifact per case, heavy for ~724 throwaway contests. Contest worlds are expressed as founder specs with genome hex so a later rung can port them. Plan unchanged.

## Cost

724 runs (484 contests, 220 solo, 20 depletion), 300 steps, 32x32. About 1290 CPU-seconds. On a heavily loaded machine (load average 25-80, 8 workers) `--write` took 219 s and 396 s, `--replay-check` 466 s; the "under about 5 minutes" budget is met only on an unloaded machine (about 160 s ideal on 8 cores). Not qualified under load. Cuts per plan 3.7 if needed: rare arms to one position and fewer seeds first.

## Commands and results

- `pnpm typecheck`: pass.
- `pnpm vitest run packages/metrics/test/opponents.test.ts apps/lab/test/opponents-render.test.ts tools/test/opponents-evidence.test.ts apps/lab/test/opponents-packaging.test.ts`: 4 files, 43 tests pass.
- `node tools/opponents/assay.ts --check`: ok. `--replay-check --jobs 8`: ok (7:45 wall under load).
- `pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts`: 41 pass.
- `git diff --stat HEAD -- packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu packages/schema experiments`: empty.
- Browser (vite dev, playwright): /opponents/ at 1280 and 375 px, no console errors, no horizontal overflow at 375, rare toggle works. One double-escaping bug found and fixed.

3-## A research programme for browser-life
4-
5-**1 November 2026 · Planning, not experimental results.** Human edition: [report.html](report.html). Agent edition: [report.md](report.md). Repository inspected at `c975f83492a50d2156faf5f3d136ea284ff2f9e5`, with existing unrelated local changes preserved. Source access was checked during this research; inaccessible material is explicitly identified below. No simulations, model API jobs, deployments, or changes to physics were performed.
6-
7-The best next move is a **museum of useful possibilities**: an archive of distinct, replayable behaviours, followed by interventions that establish what causes them. Add a historical-opponent assay to distinguish improvement from turnover. Only then spend engineering effort on a different substrate or a more expressive genome.
8-
9:This recommendation combines ASAL's learned search interface, MAP-Elites' preservation of alternatives, Stanley and Lehman's stepping stones, and Digital Red Queen's historical opponents. It also addresses a particular local problem: the repository's own foundations work found that visible change, lineage births, recovery, and reproduction cannot be treated as interchangeable evidence.
10-
11-### Three things to start
12-
13-1. **I01 — Behaviour atlas.** Extend the existing archive with carefully separated search descriptors and independent capability tests. Preserve stepping stones instead of selecting only one impressive survivor.
14-2. **I02 — Causal naturalist.** Let an observer nominate an experiment and earn credibility by predicting its result. Keep attractive pictures and biological evidence as separate outputs.
15:3. **I03 — Historical-opponent ecology.** Reuse competition and transfer machinery to ask whether later populations actually improve against a frozen panel, or merely cycle through matchups.
16-
17-### Three things to resist
18-
19-- “More novelty” as a single objective. Randomness, flicker, rendering changes and label churn can all increase apparent novelty.
20-- Another injury-selection sweep before resolving existing recovery-assay questions. The October 24 recovery programme already has a selected parent–mutant candidate and a staged plan.
21-- Treating external optimisation as autonomous evolution. A trained NCA, an LLM-written warrior and a breeder-selected pond can be excellent experiments, but the source of design must stay visible.
22-
23-## Reading corrections and evidence boundaries
24-
25-The item called **“FEP paper”** links to **Questioning Representational Optimism in Deep Learning: The Fractured Entangled Representation Hypothesis**. This is FER, not the free-energy principle. It motivates representation and controller-transfer tests here; this report does not derive an active-inference ecology from the mistaken label. [FER paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11581)
26-
27-Digital Red Queen reports increasing generality under its tests, alongside convergence and reduced behavioural diversity. Its lesson is to measure arms-race dynamics and generalisation separately from repertoire growth. It does not establish perpetual open-ended evolution. [DRQ paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03335)
28-
29-ASAL supplies externally learned criteria for searching simulations. Growing NCA supplies externally trained local rules. Assembly theory supplies a proposed account of selection through construction and multiplicity. None establishes that this repository's inferred components are reproducing individuals. Claims in the source dossier below are distinguished from the experiments proposed here.
30-
31-The two separately linked videos remain full-content access gaps. The RESCRIPT interview transcript was recovered through the browser; its short excerpts from other interviews do not close those gaps. Accessible primary papers support technical claims. The transcript also explicitly distinguishes a memorised developmental movie from a causal evolutionary sequence—a useful warning for every proposed gallery. [Interview transcript](https://app.rescript.info/public/share/Qfv3T0EVzqOXL_CYYeRr9Blv8HnNm4JsXBh7ByFjJbc)
32-

37-| `packages/search/src/mapelites.ts`, `evaluate.ts`, `bootstrap.ts`; `tools/bootstrap.ts` | Genome archive; mass/speed descriptors; viability, regeneration and light-dependence screens; configurable scoring and gates | Extend this system. Do not propose MAP-Elites as missing. |
38-| `packages/schema/src/presets.ts`, `apps/lab/src/worlds.ts` | Rotating/wandering suns, ownership variants, declared-cell sandboxes, recurring wounds, kernel-shape variation and breeder ponds | Reuse these as interventions and counterexamples; do not rename existing presets as new inventions. |
39-| `packages/lineage/src/genotype.ts` | Genotype identity, mutation replay and controller probing, including optional gene-related loci | Begin FER-inspired work with probes and edit/revert tests before redesigning the controller. |
40-| `packages/metrics/src/capabilities.ts`; `packages/runner/src/discovery.ts`; `tools/discovery.ts` | Exact-ledger observers and CPU discovery campaign freeze/run/replay/validate/reduce | Use its evidence format and execution path where supported. Novel assays need explicit schema/runner extensions. |
41-| `apps/lab/src/naturalist*.ts`; `tools/lib/bell.ts`; `tools/naturalist-envelope.ts` | Existing neutral-baseline anomaly detection and suggestions | A bell is a calibrated departure on specific worlds, not a discovery of life. New presets do not inherit its baseline. |
42-| `docs/plan.md`, especially foundations/reset notes | Recorded failures and limits of lineage, novelty, role and entity measurements | Calibrate detectors and distinguish genomic from organisational inheritance before making stronger claims. |
43-| `docs/recovery-accessibility-2026-10-24/PLAN.md`, `docs/recovery-improvement-prior-art-2026-10-24.md` | Existing retrospective audit and a staged recovery-improvement experiment | I04 builds around that decision; it does not silently replace its candidate or thresholds. |
44-| `docs/evolvability-discovery-2026-10-04/DESIGN.md` | Construction/opportunity experiments with explicit material budgets and transfer controls | I06 develops this line instead of claiming niche construction is a new local idea. |
45-
46-The default ecology infers individuals. Optional `own-cell*` sandboxes explicitly assign cell identities and birth mutations. Both exist, but evidence from a declared-cell sandbox must not be presented as emergence in the default physics. Current configuration constants are `RULE_VERSION = 1`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = 3`, `METRICS_VERSION = 2`; their presence does not make every optional configuration scientifically interchangeable.
47-
48-## Portfolio and order
49-
50-Effort bands are planning estimates for a smallest credible prototype, not delivery promises: **S** roughly 2–4 engineering days; **M** 1–2 weeks; **L** 3–6+ weeks. They exclude scientific run time, review and resolving failed measurement gates. Priority reflects expected information gained per implementation effort, not likelihood of a positive biological result.
51-

55-| I02 | Naturalist that predicts interventions | Existing observer | First / M | Fixed intervention fixtures |
56:| I03 | Historical-opponent ecology | Existing assay | First / M | Competition sensitivity without extinction floors |
57-| I04 | Repair across unfamiliar wounds | Existing assay | Next / S–M | Existing recovery programme's measurement gate |

69-## Detailed idea plans
70-
71-### I01 — Behaviour atlas and stepping stones
72-
73-**Question.** Does retaining diverse viable behaviours expose transferable capabilities that a recovery-focused archive misses? **Sources:** [MAP-Elites](https://arxiv.org/abs/1504.04909), [ASAL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17799), [Why Greatness Cannot Be Planned](https://link.springer.com/book/10.1007/978-3-319-15524-1).
74-
75-**Experience.** A browsable cabinet of short replay clips: moving, resting, repairing, following, building, coexisting. Each specimen has a reproducible starting state, an ancestry of *search proposals*, and a card saying which tests it has actually passed. People can retain an unusual specimen without declaring it fitter.
76-
77-**Smallest implementation.** Add an opt-in archive adapter beside the existing mass/speed map. Start with two predeclared physical descriptors, such as centre-of-mass displacement and temporal occupancy persistence, whose definitions are valid even when component identity is ambiguous. Evaluate an additional learned trajectory embedding as a separate arm, rather than silently replacing the descriptors. Fix rendering, frame cadence, encoder version and preprocessing. Store all accepted candidates and proposal ancestry, not just current elites. Reuse bootstrap evaluation and replay bundles; do not put model calls inside `step()`.
78-
79-**Experiment.** Equal evaluation budgets for random viable sampling, the existing archive, a physical-descriptor archive and an embedding archive. Use the same starting candidate pool. Select on screening seeds; compare on fresh environments and seeds using capabilities withheld from every selector. Run multiple independent search histories; the search history is the unit of replication, not its hundreds of correlated archive members. Report both simulator evaluations and wall time/model cost.
80-
81-**Readouts and controls.** Held-out capability coverage, archive retention, failure/extinction, duplicate-genotype rate, and transfer. A new visual bin is not a new capability. Include recoloured/re-rendered copies of the same trajectory, repeated trajectories, static patterns and noise as embedding controls. Use a fixed audit panel to measure semantic-score drift.
82-
83-**Advance/stop.** Advance only if a predefined improvement in held-out coverage survives fresh searches and render perturbations. If visual breadth rises without capability breadth, ship it as a discovery gallery and stop claiming scientific improvement. Existing held-out ecology/multilevel metrics remain excluded from search unless a separately registered experiment changes their role. **Deliverable:** archive schema, comparison runner, replay gallery and audited selection ledger.
84-
85-### I02 — A naturalist that predicts interventions
86-
87-**Question.** Can a model identify a causal mechanism well enough to predict an intervention, beyond what numerical baselines predict? **Sources:** [ASAL](https://pub.sakana.ai/asal/), [FER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11581).
88-
89-**Experience.** A field note says, “I expect this patch to lose its advantage if signal is disabled,” with a confidence and a proposed paired replay. The notebook subsequently records whether the prediction survived. Observations, hypotheses and measured outcomes are visibly distinct.
90-
91-**Implementation.** Extend the existing naturalist bench and suggestion pipeline. Its input is a fixed evidence envelope: registered frame samples, census series, configuration and an allowed intervention menu. Its output is structured data containing subject/time, predicted direction, readout, intervention, confidence and an abstention option. A host validator permits only predefined data operations. Use the discovery campaign format where compatible; the current CPU case runner is not automatically a general naturalist benchmark.
92-
93-**Experiment.** Blind the proposer to intervention outcomes. Compare numeric-only, video-only, combined inputs, a fixed rule proposer and shuffled evidence on identical cases. Split whole source histories between development and final testing. Do not let multiple windows from the same run leak across splits. Evaluate on already-known mechanism fixtures first, then unfamiliar cases.
94-
95-**Readouts.** Proper probability scoring (Brier score), directional accuracy, abstention/coverage, invalid proposals, outcome effect size, cost per useful experiment and counterfactual agreement. Equalise available evidence and intervention budget. An expensive model must improve decisions, not merely write more interesting descriptions.
96-
97-**Advance/stop.** Require better prospective prediction than the strongest simple baseline and stable calibration on held-out histories. If it only captions, retain a clearly labelled narration feature. Do not extend existing neutral-bell calibration to new worlds without recalibration. **Deliverable:** prediction/outcome JSONL, comparison table and a notebook view of failed as well as successful predictions.
98-
99:### I03 — Historical-opponent ecology
100-
101-**Question.** Does later evolution produce broader competitive ability, specialised counter-strategies, or mere replacement? **Sources:** [Digital Red Queen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03335), [Core War](https://corewar.co.uk/).
102-
103-**Implementation.** Archive genotypes or explicitly defined population samples at fixed times, then compete them against a frozen panel spanning ancestral and intermediate opponents. Reuse mutation replay and discovery tooling. Begin with genome-only, standard-inoculum common gardens; test ecological context separately. Moving a whole patch also moves stored resources, partners and developmental state, so that is a different experiment.
104-
105-**Experiment.** Use reciprocal positions and starting ratios, mutation-off assays, independent source histories, identical budgets and a genotype-neutral two-label control. Start from a small panel of known distinct strategies to establish assay sensitivity. Include isolated-growth performance so “wins” caused solely by general vigour are distinguishable from matchup-specific advantages. If evolving against an archive is later tested, compare recent-only, fixed-panel and historical-mixture opponents at matched compute.
106-
107-**Readouts.** Full pairwise payoff matrices with uncertainty, invasion from rare, extinction, generality on opponents withheld from training, and cycles such as A beats B, B beats C, C beats A. Report diversity independently from mean win rate. Account for synthesis versus lottery relabelling where the exact observer permits it; label unresolved attribution rather than claiming predation.
108-
109-**Advance/stop.** Do not run a long tournament if both candidates routinely hit the extinction floor. First fix the assay or report it uninformative. A positive result establishes competitive change in the tested environment, not organism reproduction or open-endedness. **Deliverable:** replayable matchup bundles and an interactive time-versus-opponent matrix. No predation rule is required for the first test.
110-
111-### I04 — Repair across unfamiliar wounds
112-
113-**Question.** Is a recovery advantage specific to one lesion geometry, or does it transfer to unseen damage? **Sources:** [Growing NCA](https://distill.pub/2020/growing-ca/), [Lenia](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.05433).
114-
115-**Implementation.** Continue the existing October 24 measurement and parent–mutant programme first. That plan already fixes its candidate and staged decision; this idea is a separately recorded follow-up after its outcome, not permission to replace it. Add replayable damage masks and a secondary repair-characterisation battery around the current evaluator.
116-
117-**Experiment.** Match removed bound mass across contiguous, dispersed and asymmetric damage, rather than equating radius with injury severity. Include sham intervention, matched intact worlds, mutation-off trials, and a background-regrowth control. Fix damage times before outcomes; use fresh challenge geometries for final transfer. Account for the destination of removed matter and energy through the existing lesion semantics. A wound is an external intervention, not autonomous ecology.
118-
119-**Readouts.** Recovery time, retained-component continuity under the operational tracker, spatial organisation, resumed function, and mass. Report whole-tile replenishment separately from remnant recovery; a film that fills space can pass a mass score without repairing the original organisation. Censored recovery times and extinct trials remain in the analysis.
120-
121-**Advance/stop.** Advance from reliable recovery measurement to transfer, then to a selection experiment only if both are informative. A failure of identity tracking is a measurement-limited result. A successful engineered NCA (I10) is a positive control for an observer, not proof that current ecology can evolve repair. **Deliverable:** damage-mask fixtures, a mass-severity audit, and side-by-side repair trajectories. It should explain existing recovery results before generating another wound sweep.
122-
123-### I05 — Controller locality and useful mutations
124-
125-**Question.** Are useful edits rare because the local neural controller entangles unrelated behaviours? **Sources:** [FER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11581), [AutoML-Zero](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.03384).
126-
127-**Implementation.** Start without new physics: use `probeGenome`, genotype keys and exact mutation/reversion to map perturbations onto controller outputs and independently assayed capabilities. Separate unchanged/clamped edits from genuine genotype changes. The FER hypothesis concerns deep learned representations; its application to a small evolved fixed-point controller is an analogy to test, not an established diagnosis.
128-
129-**Experiment.** Evaluate a fixed mutation neighbourhood for selected viable genomes on a predefined sensor grid and physical scenarios. Compare single-locus and matched-magnitude multi-locus edits; stratify by locus and starting genome. Only after identifying a bottleneck build a separate modular-controller variant. Match parameter count where feasible, mutation opportunity, initialization quality and compute. Test direct search and transfer separately so “modularity helps” is not just a better initial founder.
130-
131-**Readouts.** Fraction of viable edits; functional improvement; collateral loss in other tasks; reversibility; transfer across light/nutrient settings. Actuator response locality is a mechanistic descriptor, not itself fitness. Use neutral/silent edits and shuffled sensor labels as controls; the latter is a negative fixture, not a valid ecological intervention.
132-
133-**Advance/stop.** If the existing architecture admits a broad useful neighbourhood, prefer better search and ecological incentives to a genome redesign. If a modular variant helps only its trained tasks, report that limit. Any controller-layout change needs versioned encoding, migration decisions and CPU/WGSL parity. **Deliverable:** mutation-effect matrix and a go/no-go argument for a separately versioned architecture.
134-
135-### I06 — Construction that opens another niche
136-
137-**Question.** Can one variant change the environment so that another gains a capability, and can that dependency survive fresh tests? **Sources:** [Assembly theory](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-023-06600-9), [Lenia](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.05433), [stepping stones](https://link.springer.com/book/10.1007/978-3-319-15524-1).
138-
139-**Implementation.** Extend the existing discovery design's conditioned-field experiments. Start with polymer, chemical fields and current transport rules. A builder conditions a world; a receiver is tested in that field. No new organism abstraction or predation actuator is needed. The “second niche” is an assay outcome, not a named ecological role assigned by code.
140-
141-**Experiment.** Builder, nonbuilder and cost-retaining ablation start with the same budgets. Compare whole conditioned fields, matched-inventory spatial reconstructions and receiver-alone inoculum controls. Explicitly distinguish builder-present co-culture from builder-removed transfer. Reserve the receiver inoculum inside the initial matter/energy budget; do not overwrite occupied cells or erase polymer without recording transformed material and energy.
142-
143-**Readouts.** Receiver survival, growth, measured flux use and spatial dependence; builder cost; inventories before/after transfer. Exact per-step flux attribution requires non-saturation and adequate sampling. A census sample cannot be integrated as though it recorded every intervening reaction.
144-
145-**Advance/stop.** First establish a causal environmental benefit on fresh seeds. Next ask whether accessible mutations generate the builder and receiver. A later A+B→C capability test must predeclare C and compare A alone, B alone, A+B and resource-matched controls. More biomass after conditioning alone does not establish construction; more initial stored energy can explain it. **Deliverable:** budget-audited transfers, two-stage causal assay and a dependency graph with supported and unsupported edges.
146-
147-### I07 — Construction grammar and copy number
148-
149-**Question.** Can repeated, difficult-to-construct structures distinguish selected organisation from noise in these simulations? **Source:** [Assembly theory](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-023-06600-9).
150-
151-**Implementation.** Build a read-only, explicitly *assembly-inspired* observer. Define primitives, allowed joining operations, object boundaries, rotation/translation equivalence and copy identity before computing a score. Start with exact small synthetic objects whose minimal construction is enumerable. For large lattice patches use a bounded grammar search and label its output an upper bound or heuristic, not an exact assembly index. Compression ratio is not automatically assembly index.
152-
153-**Experiment.** Calibrate against repeated tiles, deterministic crystals, random fields, random fields with duplicated motifs, manually assembled hierarchies and evolving worlds. Match occupied mass and observation resolution. Compare reconstruction cost and multiplicity separately before considering any combined statistic. Test sensitivity to segmentation and temporal sampling.
154-
155-**Readouts.** Construction-length estimate/bound, copy count, persistence, uncertainty in equivalence classes, and incremental predictive value over simple compression and occupancy. Add destruction/reconstruction interventions to connect the measured organisation to a capability.
156-
157-**Advance/stop.** Reject a life-detection claim if duplicated random motifs score as strongly as the proposed positive cases. If object definitions dominate conclusions, publish the sensitivity failure rather than tuning boundaries until treatment wins. Do not optimise this metric and then use it as held-out evidence. **Deliverable:** grammar specification, exact toy tests, streaming patch sampler and an observer calibration report. This is a promising measurement project, not a settled solution to detecting selection.
158-
159-### I08 — Collective sensing under moving light
160-
161-**Question.** Does local interaction improve navigation under unfamiliar light trajectories, beyond independent responses or a hard-coded heading? **Sources:** [Boids](https://www.red3d.com/cwr/boids/), [Lenia](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.05433).
162-
163-**Implementation.** Begin with `planet-followers`, `planet-sensing`, `planet-wander` and the existing signal/adhesion controls. These already contain blind and sensing strategies. A new experiment changes the light schedule and comparison design; a Boids-like alignment actuator is a later rule fork only if needed. Do not call existing signal-gradient following a new flocking mechanism.
164-
165-**Experiment.** Fixed-speed sun during screening; reversed, paused and wandering light during transfer. Match light exposure budgets as far as possible and report differences. Compare isolated and grouped inocula, signal on/off, motility on/off, and blind drifters. Separate signal production cost from information where supported; any cost-preserving replacement must be specified and ledger-checked.
166-
167-**Readouts.** Light capture per input energy, tracking lag, group cohesion, individual motion and survival. Whole-field drift or common environmental forcing can create apparent collective motion, so measure residual coordination against independent agents exposed to the same sun. Assess whether a benefit follows group composition across a common-garden transfer.
168-
169-**Advance/stop.** A group-level improvement requires a causal interaction benefit exceeding matched independent response. Schooling appearance alone is a visual success, not higher-level individuality. **Deliverable:** controlled navigation benchmark and a public world where a visitor can reverse the sun and compare blinded strategies.
170-
171-### I09 — Conservative particle ecology
172-
173-**Question.** Do discrete moving material units make mixing, boundaries and lineage continuity easier to measure than the current field? **Source:** [Particle Life](https://github.com/tom-mohr/particle-life).
174-
--
278-4. **Bounded pilot.** Benchmark runtime and calibration, choose the protocol, freeze held-out inputs, then collect scientific data. Do not tune on confirmation outcomes.
279-5. **Independent read-only review.** Use `gpt-6.1-sol` at high effort per `AGENTS.md`, at phase boundaries and before commits. Resolve evidence and interpretation errors as well as code defects.
280-6. **Human result.** Publish a replay, effect/uncertainty, failed cases and a clear decision. Report implemented/local/hosted/physical-GPU evidence separately.
281-
282-Typical applicable verification commands are `pnpm typecheck`, focused `pnpm vitest run <test-file>`, `pnpm test`, and `deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts` for changes to shared dynamics. Segmentation/checkpoint changes additionally need `tests/deno/segments.ts` and `tests/deno/stitch.ts`. Use the repo's actual scripts and local instructions at implementation time; these are not claims that those tests ran during this document task. A new package must update all four alias maps listed in `AGENTS.md`.
283-
284-### Suggested sequence
285-
286-**Phase A: evidence and breadth.** Build I14's small null fixture set alongside I01's archive manifest and I02's fixed prediction envelope. The deliverable is usable replay evidence even if the learned methods add no value.
287-
288:**Phase B: causal capability.** Choose I03 or I06 according to which validated fixture is available. Continue I04 only through the existing recovery decision. Run I05's cheap controller probes before proposing a representation fork.
289-
290-**Phase C: one new world.** Choose I10 for a strong interactive repair exhibit, I11 for computational replication and competition, or I09 for material/identity measurement. Do not start all three merely because their demos are attractive. Each must answer a question the existing ecology cannot answer as cheaply.
291-
292-**Phase D: change what can evolve.** I12 and I13 become justified only after the earlier measurements locate a constraint. There is no source-backed reason to assume that additional genome capacity alone produces unlimited evolution.
293-
294-## How to read the source dossier
295-
296-The following notes cover the supplied sources by research cluster, with exact URLs and access limitations. Added primary sources are labelled supplementary. Statements about the papers describe the authors' arguments or reported experiments; idea cards above are proposals for this repository. Where a source could not be read, the report says so instead of silently substituting an abstract, another video, or a model's recollection.
297-
298-## Source dossier — Search and automated discovery
299-
300-
301-### ASAL — Kumar et al. (2024; arXiv v2 May 2025)
302-[Paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2412.17799v2), [user-supplied abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17799), [project and demos](https://pub.sakana.ai/asal/).
303-Uses foundation-model image representations for three tasks: text-target search, temporal novelty, and illumination across simulations. Tested substrates include Boids, Particle Life, life-like cellular automata, Lenia and NCA. Target search uses CLIP with Sep-CMA-ES; illumination uses a genetic search retaining diverse simulations. Temporal novelty minimizes similarity to earlier embedded frames; illumination measures nearest-neighbor difference across simulations. CLIP/DINOv2 comparisons outperform pixel representations qualitatively for human-aligned diversity. The atlas is a presentation of discovered behavior, not a biological validation. Its finite observation windows and representation-relative novelty do not establish indefinite evolution, causal individuality, heredity or ecological closure. The project page supplies useful demos but is not independent corroboration of the paper.
304-
305-### Digital Red Queen — Kumar et al. (2026)
306-[Paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.03335v1), [abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03335), [project explanation](https://sakana.ai/drq/).
307-Evolves Redcode programs against a growing archive of previous champions, using LLM mutation within MAP-Elites. Descriptors are spawned processes and memory coverage. Held-out human warriors assess generality. Across 96 runs, generality increases while behavioral diversity across histories decreases and within-history change slows. Limited-history comparisons study cycling. Removing the diversity archive hurts optimization. Crucial distinction: the paper finds competitive robustness and convergence, not indefinitely expanding ecological diversity. LLM proposals import an external prior; they are not mutations arising spontaneously inside the simulated world. A growing opponent archive also dilutes each new opponent's effect.
308-
309-### MAP-Elites — Mouret and Clune (2015)
310-[Paper](https://arxiv.org/html/1504.04909v1), [abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/1504.04909).
311-Stores high-performing solutions across a discretized, user-selected behavior space. A candidate competes within its descriptor cell rather than only for one global optimum. Cross-cell stepping stones can help reach solutions that direct optimization misses. Demonstrations include neural networks and soft robots. Descriptor coverage is conditional on chosen axes; it is not a coordinate-free measure of diversity or evidence of open-endedness. The HTML header contains an inconsistent generated preprint date; cite the arXiv submission record (20 April 2015), not that header.
312-
313-### AlphaEvolve — Novikov et al. (2025)
314-[Paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2506.13131v1), [abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13131).
315-An evolutionary coding system generates changes to designated code, executes machine-gradeable evaluators and retains useful programs in a database. Multiple scores and rich evaluator feedback support search; the user supplies the evaluation contract. Applications span mathematical constructions and computing infrastructure. Its ability to improve code does not solve the problem of choosing a valid scientific objective. This is a methodological inspiration for a local proposer/evaluator loop, not evidence that the exact AlphaEvolve service or implementation is available here.
316-
317-### AutoML-Zero — Real et al. (2020)
318-[Paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2003.03384v2), [abstract](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.03384).
319-Searches programs built from low-level mathematical instructions, with Setup, Predict and Learn components and scalar/vector/matrix memory. Regularized evolution mutates instructions, operands and whole components. Separate search, selection and final-test tasks limit overfitting; cheap proxy tasks and functional-equivalence checks improve throughput. The work demonstrates discovery of learning algorithms under an externally supplied training/evaluation scaffold. “From scratch” does not mean no design assumptions or emergent organism reproduction. Its floating-point execution and supervised-learning interfaces are not drop-in replacements for this repo's bounded integer controllers.
320-
321-### Akarsh Kumar's website
322-[Homepage](https://akarshkumar.com/).
323-Verified as an author-maintained index linking ASAL, DRQ and the Fractured Entangled Representation (FER) hypothesis. Useful for provenance and follow-up paper discovery, not a separate empirical result. It describes interests in open-ended systems, environment generation and self-play. It also links the Stanley/FER MLST interview supplied by the user; consult the primary FER paper for technical claims rather than infer them from the documentary title.
324-
325-
326-## Source dossier — Theory, representation and computational life
327-
328-
329-### T1 — Assembly theory: read via authors' institutional PDF
330-
331-Original: <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-023-06600-9>
332-Accessible paper: <https://eprints.gla.ac.uk/305433/1/305433.pdf>
333-Title: *Assembly theory explains and quantifies selection and evolution* (Sharma, Czégel, Lachmann, Kempes, Walker, Cronin, 2023).
334-
335-The paper combines an object's shortest recursive construction path (assembly index) with copy abundance. Its ensemble measure is `A = sum_i exp(a_i) (n_i - 1) / N_T`. The authors model discovery and production in assembly spaces; crucially their selection model specifies differential reuse rather than modelling every functional mechanism. Application requires physically meaningful primitives and combination operations. This suggests measuring repeated construction rather than treating visual intricacy or compression as evidence of life. It does not establish that any compression measure, arbitrary image motif count, or high index proves Darwinian evolution. Nature's direct fetch redirected to inaccessible authorization; the University of Glasgow PDF supplied the paper. For a counterpoint by a research author see <https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00239-024-10163-2> (title: *Assembly Theory: What It Does and What It Does Not Do*); it disputes expansive evolutionary interpretations.
336-
337-### T2 — The supplied “FEP” URL is FER, not the free-energy principle
338-
339-<https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11581>
340-Full text: <https://arxiv.org/html/2505.11581v1>
341-Title: *Questioning Representational Optimism in Deep Learning: The Fractured Entangled Representation Hypothesis* (Kumar, Clune, Lehman, Stanley, 2025).
342-
343-A position paper comparing evolved Picbreeder CPPNs with SGD networks reproducing the same images. Output similarity can mask different internal organization. The authors use neuron visualizations and perturbations to motivate fractured/entangled versus unified/factored representations. Picbreeder combines human selection, historical open-ended search and NEAT topology growth. The paper explicitly leaves the relative contributions of the algorithm and objective-free search unresolved. It is not evidence that evolution always gives modular control, or that this project's fixed-point network has FER. Do not add an active-inference/FEP project based on this mislabeled URL. The relevant experiment here is representation locality, mutation locality and transfer under matched capacities and histories.
344-
345-### T3 — Why Greatness Cannot Be Planned: publisher preview, not entire book
346-
347-<https://link.springer.com/book/10.1007/978-3-319-15524-1>
348-Kenneth O. Stanley and Joel Lehman, 2015. Publisher overview and chapter list accessed; full book subscription text was not read. Its central argument challenges narrowly objective-driven routes to innovation and favors discovery through serendipitous stepping stones. Use as a motivation for retaining diverse reusable candidates, not as a theorem that objectives or preregistered scientific tests are harmful. Search can be exploratory while evaluation remains rigorous.
349-
350-### T4 — Computational irreducibility: specified page read
351-
352-<https://www.wolframscience.com/nks/p737--computational-irreducibility/>
353-Wolfram contrasts analytic prediction that bypasses trajectories with systems whose behavior lacks such known shortcuts. This is an authorial conceptual argument; it is not a proof that browser-life cannot be accelerated or forecast. Test the useful operational claim: which observables can be predicted at which horizons, and where must the simulator actually be run? Poor prediction alone is not a measure of life, intelligence or open-endedness.
354-
355-### T5 — MLST Stanley video: unavailable full video/transcript
356-
357-<https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1q6Hhz0MAg>
358-YouTube fetch throttled. First-party MLST archive bibliography identifies this URL as a FER introduction, with an alternate title *AI is SO Smart, Why Are Its Internals ‘Spaghetti’?*: <https://archive.mlst.ai/read/why-creativity-cannot-be-interpolated> . This identifies the topic but is not the requested video's transcript. Use T2 as the primary technical source. No timestamp-specific video claims verified.
359-
360-### T6 — MLST Blaise video: unavailable full video/transcript
361-
362-<https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rMSEqJ_4EBk>
363-YouTube fetch throttled. No verified full transcript obtained. Treat interview as a pointer to the research, not an independently inspected evidentiary source. Supplementary primary paper below supplies technical grounding, and a later same-team paper materially changes the interpretation.

410-**Transfer:** group-level motion metrics and controlled perception experiments. Do not call a direct Boids port emergent individuality: individual boids are built into that substrate.

412-### Particle Life — Tom Mohr
413-Read supplied repo: <https://github.com/tom-mohr/particle-life> . It is a Java simulation framework; README explicitly says development moved to <https://github.com/tom-mohr/particle-life-app> and this repository no longer receives updates. Read implementation <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tom-mohr/particle-life/main/src/main/java/com/particle_life/Physics.java> : nearby pairs use a type-indexed interaction matrix, friction and velocity integration; spatial containers restrict neighbor scans. Matrix reciprocity is not enforced by this update. This is a framework, so the accelerator implementation also determines the exact force law. Inspect that before attempting faithful reproduction.
414-
415-**Transfer:** compare reciprocal versus asymmetric interactions, stable clusters versus persistent exchange. Conserving particle count alone does not establish the repo's matter/energy ecology or heredity. Original Java code is GPL-3.0; a conceptually inspired implementation should not silently copy it into incompatible project licensing.

417-### Core War
418-Supplied <https://corewar.co.uk/> and the attempted standards page returned 403 through browsing. Alternative practitioner-authored technical tutorial read: <https://www.corewars.org/docs/guide.html> . It describes Redcode programs in circular shared memory, execution by MARS, relative addresses, process queues, copying and process termination. The tutorial identifies its dialect as the ICWS '94 draft with pMARS extensions. A copied instruction and a new execution process are distinguishable events. Classic Core War gives each competing program its own queue, so organism boundaries and scheduling entitlement are partly declared by the runtime.
419-
420:**Transfer:** exact, inspectable causal histories of instruction copying; adversarial evaluation against historical opponents. A custom energy-budget VM is a new substrate, not a faithful Core War benchmark, and must be labelled accordingly.

    10	export const sha256 = (v: string | Uint8Array): string => createHash("sha256").update(v).digest("hex");
    11	export function canonical(value: unknown): string {
    12	  if (Array.isArray(value)) return `[${value.map(canonical).join(",")}]`;
    13	  if (value && typeof value === "object") {
    14	    const o = value as Record<string, unknown>;
    15	    return `{${Object.keys(o).sort().map((k) => `${JSON.stringify(k)}:${canonical(o[k])}`).join(",")}}`;
    16	  }
    17	  return JSON.stringify(value);
    18	}
